world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP. The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP, is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

world
workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -
quirements. This forces companies to train a considerable number of
young graduates themselves before they are ready for the workforce.
According to the Telecom Talent in the 5G Era report by the Telecom
Sector Skill Council, only 40% of IT and telecommunications graduates
are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026
Johanna MELKA
johanna.melka@bnpparibas.com

### Page 7

Tarik Rharrab
Data scientist +33 1 43 16 95 56 tarik.rharrab@bnpparibas.com

## Document 366: Metals sell-off runs ahead of physical markets

It has been a diﬃcult week for metals. Copper is down more
than 8%, aluminium almost 6% and gold around 9% as a
stronger dollar, higher rate expectations and weaker risk
appetite weigh on the complex
Ewa Manthey
Metals have come under sharp pressure this week, despite continued tightness
in physical markets
The speed of the sell-oﬀ looks more like a sharp repricing of investor
exposure than a broad weakening in physical markets. Copper has
taken the biggest hit as investors unwind bullish positions and

reassess AI-related demand. Yet the sharp correction in prices
contrasts with still-tight physical markets.

Investor selling weighs on copper
Copper is on track for one of its steepest weekly declines in a
decade.
We think the speed of the sell-oﬀ partly reﬂects an unwind of bullish
investor positioning. Both LME and COMEX net length have fallen
sharply from recent highs, while positioning across the wider
metals complex has also become less bullish.

Copper has also become more closely correlated with equity
markets, including AI-related stocks. The AI link matters for investor
sentiment, but it does not make copper's demand story dependent
on data centres alone. Grid investment and electriﬁcation remain
important sources of demand. But as investors cut exposure to AI-
related assets, copper has become more vulnerable to the same
selling pressure.

Copper could remain under pressure if funds continue to cut
positions or the macro backdrop deteriorates. But the price
correction has moved much faster than physical conditions. Tight
availability in China should provide some support once the investor
selling eases.
The risk is that more US metal returns to the wider market if tariﬀ
expectations fade further, gradually easing some of the regional
tightness now supporting the physical market.
Aluminium supply remains tight for now

Aluminium has fallen with the wider complex, but nearby supply
remains tight. The LME cash-to-three-month spread has moved
into a steep backwardation, while LME stocks remain historically
low and largely of Russian origin, limiting what is readily available
to some buyers. This should help contain the near-term downside.
At the same time, investors have reduced bullish aluminium
positions from recent highs, in line with the broader move across
metals.

The medium-term picture is less supportive. Middle Eastern
production is recovering, and new Indonesian capacity is coming
online. We expect the market to move towards surplus next year,
which should limit the scope for a sustained price recovery.

Gold feels the pressure from higher rates
Gold has fallen for ﬁve straight sessions and is down around 9%

this week, putting it on course for its steepest weekly decline since
March. Prices have also touched their lowest level since September
2025.
The shift in the macro backdrop has weighed on gold, with higher
rate expectations and a stronger dollar reducing its appeal. The
equity sell-oﬀ may also have prompted some investors to sell gold
to raise cash.
We expect gold to remain vulnerable while rate expectations and
the dollar remain elevated. Central bank demand remains an

important longer-term support, with China adding 26 tonnes to its
reserves in October. But for now, macro forces are likely to set the
direction.

## Document 378: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors (see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non-AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to momentum that is both solid and broad-based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

3Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,066 tỷ VND (-6% yoy) và LNST công ty mẹ 
đạt 2,206 tỷ VND (-14% yoy). Lợi nhuận suy giảm chủ yếu do (1) khấu hao tăng từ 
các nhà ga mới và (2) ghi nhận lỗ tỷ giá 168 tỷ VND trong kỳ, trái ngược với khoản lãi 
tỷ giá cùng kỳ 2025. Lũy kế 9M2026, doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ lần lượt đạt 
19,226/7,435 tỷ VND (+0%/+2% yoy).

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa qua hệ thống ACV tăng trưởng bình 
quân 7%/5.5%/năm đến 2030, thấp hơn toàn ngành do (1) sân bay Phú Quốc 
không còn thuộc hệ thống ACV từ đầu 2026 và (2) một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội 
Bài dịch chuyển tự nhiên sang sân bay Gia Bình khi đi vào khai thác. Mức độ dịch 
chuyển trước năm 2030 dự kiến còn hạn chế do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ hoàn thiện 
sân bay và hạ tầng giao thông kết nối.

Cảng HKQT Long Thành giai đoạn 1 dự kiến khai thác một phần từ cuối tháng 
12/2026 và bắt đầu tiếp nhận các chuyến bay quốc tế đường dài từ Tân Sơn Nhất 
vào ngày 28/03/2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV. Tuy nhiên, 
tốc độ chuyển dịch có thể chậm hơn kế hoạch do hạ tầng kết nối chưa hoàn thiện, 
trong khi chi phí khấu hao và vận hành tăng dự kiến gây áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận 
của ACV giai đoạn 2027–2028.

Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền, chúng tôi duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với ACV với giá mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng 
giá 4.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 18/11/2026.

Biểu đồ 6. Các quốc gia có lượng khách đến Việt Nam lớn nhất trong 10M2026 (khách) 
Trung Quốc và Hàn Quốc tiếp tục là hai thị 
trường khách quốc tế lớn nhất đến Việt Nam 
trong 10M2026 nhưng tăng trưởng hạn chế 
(+1.4% và -6.3% yoy), chủ yếu chịu tác động 
từ giá nhiên liệu tăng cao, khiến các hãng 
hàng không cắt giảm tần suất bay và du 
khách có xu hướng ưu tiên các điểm đến gần. 
Ngược lại, khách châu Âu tăng mạnh (+51.5% 
yoy), chủ yếu nhờ (1) thị trường Nga phục hồi 
(+150% yoy) sau khi các đường bay thẳng và 
charter được khôi phục, mở rộng và (2) hưởng 
lợi từ chính sách miễn thị thực bổ sung cho 12 
quốc gia châu Âu từ tháng 8/2025 và việc tăng 
cường kết nối hàng không trong năm 2026.

Nguồn: GSO

Triển vọng tăng trưởng hành khách 
tích cực, song ACV dự kiến tăng 
chậm hơn toàn ngành 
 Theo dự báo của Bộ Xây dựng, lượng hành khách thông qua các cảng hàng 
không Việt Nam dự kiến tăng trưởng bình quân 9.7%/năm giai đoạn 2026–
2030, đạt 191.2 triệu lượt vào năm 2030. Về dài hạn, Airbus dự báo lưu lượng 
hành khách hàng không Việt Nam tăng trưởng 7.7%/năm trong 20 năm tới, gần 
gấp khoảng 2 lần mức trung bình toàn cầu (3.9%/năm). Các động lực chính bao 
gồm triển vọng tăng trưởng kinh tế, nhu cầu du lịch gia tăng, chính sách miễn 
thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng đường bay quốc tế, cùng kế hoạch bổ 
sung đội bay của các hãng hàng không.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế và nội địa của ACV tăng trưởng bình 
quân lần lượt 7% và 5.5%/năm đến 2030, thấp hơn mức tăng trưởng chung của 
toàn ngành do (1) sân bay Phú Quốc được chuyển ra khỏi hệ thống ACV từ đầu 
2026 và (2) một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội Bài dịch chuyển tự nhiên sang Gia 
Bình khi sân bay này đi vào khai thác. Chúng tôi đánh giá mức độ dịch chuyển 
trước năm 2030 còn hạn chế do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ xây dựng sân bay và hạ 
tầng kết nối. Bên cạnh đó, đề xuất chuyển các chuyến bay quốc tế từ Nội Bài 
sang Gia Bình hiện chưa được phê duyệt và chưa được phản ánh trong dự 
phóng. Nếu được thông qua, đề xuất này có thể ảnh hưởng đáng kể đến lợi 
nhuận của ACV, do lợi nhuận trên mỗi khách quốc tế cao gấp khoảng 4–5 lần 
khách nội địa, đặc biệt trong giai đoạn sau năm 2030.

Nguồn: ACV, KBSV tổng hợp  Nguồn: ACV, KBSV tổng hợp

Cảng HKQT Long Thành mở ra dư 
địa tăng trưởng dài hạn, song tạo 
áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận trong 
ngắn và trung hạn 
 Cảng HKQT Long Thành dự kiến khai thác một phần từ cuối tháng 12/2026, chủ 
yếu phục vụ các chuyến bay nội địa và một số chuyến quốc tế. Hoạt động 
chuyển các chuyến bay quốc tế đường dài và vận tải hàng hóa từ Tân Sơn Nhất 
dự kiến bắt đầu từ ngày 28/03/2027. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi đánh giá tốc độ dịch 
chuyển có thể chậm hơn kế hoạch do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ hoàn thiện hạ tầng 
giao thông kết nối với trung tâm TP.HCM, đặc biệt là dự án mở rộng cao tốc 
TP.HCM – Long Thành – Dầu Giây và các dự án kết nối đường sắt bao gồm Thủ 
Thiêm – Long Thành.

Dự án cảng HKQT Long Thành kỳ vọng sẽ mở ra dư địa tăng trưởng dài hạn cho 
ACV, dù gây áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận trong giai đoạn đầu khai thác. Việc đưa 
Long Thành vào vận hành sẽ giúp ACV mở rộng công suất, giảm áp lực quá tải 
tại Tân Sơn Nhất, đồng thời tạo điều kiện thu hút thêm các đường bay quốc tế, 
đặc biệt là đường bay dài và hoạt động vận tải hàng hóa. Tuy nhiên, trong ngắn 
và trung hạn, chi phí khấu hao và vận hành tăng trong khi sản lượng hành 
khách cần thời gian để đạt công suất tối ưu dự kiến sẽ gây áp lực lên biên lợi 
nhuận của ACV giai đoạn 2027–2028. Trong mô hình dự phóng, chúng tôi ghi 
nhận khấu hao Long Thành theo tiến độ nghiệm thu, kết chuyển và đưa vào sử 
dụng từng hạng mục tài sản cố định, thay vì toàn bộ dự án ngay khi bắt đầu vận 
hành.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, 
giá mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu  
 Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền, chúng tôi duy trì khuyến nghị 
TRUNG LẬP đối với cổ phiếu ACV trong bối cảnh rủi ro từ đề xuất chuyển các 
chuyến bay quốc tế từ Nội Bài sang Gia Bình gia tăng. Giá mục tiêu của ACV là 
39,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương với mức tăng giá tiềm năng 4.5% so với mức 
giá đóng cửa 37,800 VND/cổ phiếu ngày 18/11/2026. Các giả định chính bao 
gồm: 
- Giai đoạn 2026–2030, dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa qua hệ 
thống cảng hàng không ACV tăng trưởng bình quân 7%/5.5%/năm, 
thấp hơn tốc độ tăng trưởng chung của ngành hàng không Việt Nam, 
chủ yếu do (1) ACV không còn ghi nhận sản lượng hành khách tại sân 
bay Phú Quốc và (2) giả định một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội Bài dịch 
chuyển tự nhiên sang Gia Bình với mức độ còn hạn chế do hạ tầng kết 
nối chưa hoàn thiện. Dự phóng chưa phản ánh đề xuất chuyển các 
chuyến bay quốc tế sang Gia Bình. 
- Giá dịch vụ trung bình cảng hàng không dự kiến sẽ điều chỉnh tăng 2% 
mỗi 4 năm. 
- Dự án Long Thành giai đoạn 1 và giai đoạn 2 bắt đầu đưa vào khai thác 
từ cuối 2026 và đầu 2032, gia tăng doanh thu nhưng đồng thời tạo áp 
lực lên biên lợi nhuận trong giai đoạn đầu vận hành do chi phí khấu 
hao và vận hành tăng.

### Page 8

Rủi ro giảm đối với giá mục tiêu ACV chủ yếu đến từ khả năng đề xuất chuyển 
các chuyến bay quốc tế từ Nội Bài sang Gia Bình được phê duyệt. Kịch bản cơ 
sở hiện chỉ phản ánh mức dịch chuyển tự nhiên. Trong trường hợp đề xuất 
được thông qua, sản lượng khách quốc tế qua hệ thống ACV có thể thấp hơn 
đáng kể so với dự phóng, kéo theo suy giảm doanh thu và lợi nhuận do khả 
năng sinh lời trên mỗi khách quốc tế cao hơn khoảng 4–5 lần so với khách nội 
địa.

Biểu đồ 13. P/B, ROE lịch sử

P/B của ACV tiếp tục giảm về quanh 1.8–1.9x, 
chạm và có lúc xuống dưới ngưỡng -2std. Đây 
là vùng thấp nhất 5 năm và chiết khấu sâu so 
với mức trung bình 5 năm, phản ánh lo ngại 
về áp lực khấu hao và chi phí khi Long Thành 
đi vào vận hành.

## Document 389: The Big Picture_ Plunge Protection

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Big Picture_ Plunge Protection.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: The Big Picture: Plunge Protection
Date:    6 hours ago
The Big Picture: Plunge Protection

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
The Big Picture: Plunge
Protection
By Ed Yardeni • 23 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Ma Fo / Unsplash

I. Finding Support
The S&P 500 may be finding support around 7,000, which marked the
index's record high earlier this year, just before the Middle East war
started (chart). If so, the S&P 500 may have bottomed on Friday at
6,910.39. Yet another V-shaped rebound is also possible. Nevertheless,
we did lower our year-end target for the second month in a row on
Saturday to 7,500 from 7,900. We are still using $425 per share as our
2027 forward earnings estimate at year-end 2026. But we are lowering our

### Page 2

forward P/E estimate from 18.6 to 17.6 to reflect ongoing jitters about the
AI trade.
We still view the post-midterms selloff as a buying opportunity. We
currently project the S&P 500 will rise to 8,500 to 9,000 by the end of
2027.
We should mention that if support doesn't hold at 7,000, the next level is
the low of 6,343.72 on March 30 of this year.
The Nasdaq low on March 30, 2025 was 20,794.64.
II. Historical Perspective

### Page 3

We view the recent S&P 500 selloff as another correction in the bull
market that started on October 12, 2022 (chart).
The S&P 500 remains in a significant uptrend that began in March 2009,
marking the trough of the bear market of the Great Financial Crisis (chart).
The same holds for the Nasdaq (chart).

### Page 4

The Nasdaq has been outperforming the S&P 500 since the late 1970s
(chart).
III. Earnings
Analysts' consensus earnings estimates have been rising significantly for
2026, 2027, and 2028 since the start of this year (chart). This is a very
unusual trifecta. The previous trifecta was more muted in 2024, 2025, and
2026. The question is whether analysts' current projections are irrationally
exuberant. They might be, but we don't expect significant downward

revisions like those during the Tech Wreck of the early 2000s. Importantly,
we also don't expect a recession over the rest of the Roaring 2020s.

### Page 5

The strong upward revisions in analysts' earnings estimates for 2026-2028
are pushing S&P 500 forward earnings to record highs. That's likely to
continue if the rest of the decade remains recession-free. However, the
recent ascent in forward earnings is likely to slow, especially if industry
analysts cut their outlook for the volatile S&P 500 Semiconductor industry.
The y/y growth rate of S&P 500 forward earnings is highly correlated with
the comparable growth rate in nominal GDP (chart). The widespread

notion that the stock market is not the economy is wrong. The economy
obviously drives corporate earnings. Both are performing well.

### Page 6

IV. 2026 Versus Past 10 Years
For the S&P 500, 2026 has been much more volatile than the past 10-
year average (chart).
The same can be said for the Nasdaq (chart).

### Page 7

V. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E is now lower than it was on Liberation Day 2025
(chart). It is well below the peaks of the Tech Wreck in the early 2000s.
We don't see much more downside barring a recession.
The forward P/E of the S&P 500 Growth composite has plunged since
mid-2025 (chart).

### Page 8

Growth is very cheap. The ratio of the forward P/Es of S&P 500 Growth
and Value is down to 1.03, the lowest since the bear market of the Great
Financial Crisis (chart).

## Document 391: The ECB’s new team faces a testing start — with the skills to match (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The ECB’s new team faces a testing start — with the skills to match (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026EDITORIAL
THE ECB’S NEW TEAM FACES A TESTING START — WITH THE SKILLS TO MATCH
European leaders are set to agree on a package deal at their 26–27 November meeting for the three ECB Board seats falling
vacant in the first five months of 2027. Individually, they differ markedly from their predecessors. Collectively, they bring
a remarkably similar mix of skills. Policy preferences may be another matter. In any case, they will need to hit the ground

running. Top priorities: communications, crisis management, soft landing, balance sheet, policy-making upgrades, and role
of the euro.
MORE CONTINUITY THAN MEETS THE EYES, WITH A BONUS
Based on the nominations filed by last Friday’s deadline, EU leaders
are expected to appoint Spain’s Pablo Hernández de Cos to replace
Christine Lagarde as President in February; France’s Laurence Boone
to take over market operations from Isabel Schnabel in January; and

Germany’s Markus Brunnermeier to succeed Philip Lane as chief eco -
nomist in May.
The incoming Board members share their predecessors’ international
standing and commitment to Europe and the euro. Beyond that, the in-
dividual differences are striking. Yet together they offer the same blend
of monetary policy expertise, academic financial economics and politi-
cal nous. Monetary experience comes not from the chief economist this
time, but from Hernandez de Cos, a former Bank of Spain governor who

now heads the Bank for International Settlements, the “bank of central
banks”. Brunnermeier brings his expertise as a Princeton professor;
Boone her experience as a presidential “sherpa”, OECD chief economist
and minister for Europe. Her stints at Axa and several international
investment banks also bring an insider’s knowledge of capital markets
that the Board has lacked since Mario Draghi. All three were involved,
if not in frontline roles, in managing the Eurozone crises of the past

decade. The essential skills are there.
WILL THE ECB LEAN MORE DOVISH?
A more dovish ECB is not a foregone conclusion. Judging by his record,
I expect the new President to seek the same “owl” label as his prede -
cessor: neither dove nor hawk. Boone is likely to be as dovish as Lane.
Brunnermeier, however, is hardly a hawk — a marked contrast with the
outgoing German Board member, Schnabel. The Governing Council’s
hawks will need another champion. They will surely find at least one:

perhaps Vice-President Boris Vujcic, who succeeded Luis de Guindos
last June, or a new national central bank governor. Nature abhors a
vacuum, in ornithology as elsewhere.
NO SMOOTH SAILING AHEAD FOR THE NEW CREW
Thorny challenges await:
First, inflation is running at twice the ECB’s target, and has probably
not peaked, even if oil prices go no higher. Growth has proved sur -
prisingly resilient to rate rises, so the ever-feared trade-off between

fighting inflation and sustaining growth has yet to materialise. It will
sharpen if inflation proves stickier than expected.
Second, markets have been unsettled for weeks by fears of an AI
bubble, and of an ever-ascending path for French public debt, which
the coming spring elections could worsen. The risk premium on French
debt has hovered around twice its level at the start of the year, threate-
ning contagion to other high debt Eurozone sovereigns, notably Italy,

Belgium, and Spain. Market moves have remained orderly, and finan -
cial conditions have tightened only moderately thus far. But that may
not last.
Third, cyber disruptions, whether driven by AI agents or other sources,
have become alarmingly more frequent in recent weeks. They could
rattle the financial system, in the Eurozone or globally, at any time.
THE NEW LEADERSHIP’S PRIORITIES
The first few days
Find a sound communications footing. Many a central bank leader ce-

lebrated as a tough act to follow upon leaving office caused unintended
market volatility early in their tenure. Little prepares for the power of
every word to move markets, or for an off-the-cuff remark to undo the
effects of a carefully drafted statement. Sometimes, the lessons can be
learnt without serious consequences. Now is not such a time.
The first few weeks
- Build a new modus operandi. Each of Hernández de Cos’s prede -
cessors brought a different leadership style and skill set, and faced

different personal dynamics on the Board and Governing Council. Each
eventually made it work. That cannot be taken for granted. Efficient, ef-
fective, and well-understood decision-making — for which the current
ECB deserves high marks — takes work. With two-thirds of the Board
changing in less than a year, establishing an effective way of working
will be paramount. Fortunately, Hernandez de Cos wins high praise
from those involved in the many central banking committees he has

chaired over the years.
- Take ownership of crisis management playbooks — before a crisis
hits. Several, no doubt, exist already. The new President and Board
members will want to be comfortable with them and may add their
own touches. No crisis follows a script, but thinking through the op -
tions, trade-offs, and contingencies ahead of time can save precious
time and avert mistakes.
The first few months
- Deliver a soft landing. Both the ECB’s central forecast and econo -

mists’ consensus envisage Eurozone inflation returning close to target
by late 2027 or early 2028, with growth holding at 1–1.5%. The risks
outlined above mean that this outcome is far from assured. The ECB
will need to stay nimble.
- Finalise the balance sheet strategy. How large should the ECB’s ba -
lance sheet be in steady state, and which instruments should manage
systemic liquidity? The 2025 Strategy Review left these questions open:
there was broad agreement that unwinding crisis-era asset purchases

could stay on autopilot for a while. That phase is nearing its end. Better
to have a plan before problems arise, not least given the fraught mar-
ket environment that has settled in.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
- Consider the combined effect of monetary, balance sheet and macro-
prudential policies. A coherent, comprehensive approach would be a
major step forward and improve the odds of a soft landing. The new
Board is uniquely qualified to take it: for the first time, it will have deep
expertise and experience across all these dimensions, starting with the
President and Vice-President.

Longer term
- Understand AI’s economic effects on aggregate demand, labour mar-
kets and productivity, and the implications for monetary policy. Ex -
cellent work on this is already under way, but the effects will evolve
rapidly. Unless policymakers anticipate them, or at least understand
them in real time, monetary policy risks missing the mark. The ECB
must also clarify which effects monetary policy can address, and which
belong with fiscal authorities, such as a rise in structural unemploy -

ment, were it to occur.
- Use AI to improve monetary policy analysis and decision-making.
Brunnermeier has been at the forefront of academic economists’ work
in this area, and should be able to play a decisive role in upgrading the
ECB’s work in this regard.
- Continue to promote the euro’s international role and its place as a
pillar of EU sovereignty. That means continuing to: advocate for pro -
gress in the architecture of the European Monetary Union; modernise

euro-based cross-border payments and remain at the frontier of sup -
port for a vibrant euro-based blockchain finance ecosystem through
the ECB’s Pontes and Appia projects.
I am confident that the new leadership is as qualified as any to deliver.
Nonetheless, their task is a daunting one. All EU citizens and corpo -
rates should wish them every success. I certainly do.
Isabelle Mateos y Lago
isabelle.mateosylago@bnpparibas.com

## Document 401: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

## Document 426: Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

28 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
WHO FINANCES PRIVATE CREDIT IN THE UNITED STATES? WHAT THE FEDERAL RESERVE’S DATA REVEAL
Céline Choulet
SOURCE: FEDERAL RESERVE (ENHANCED FINANCIAL ACCOUNTS), BNP PARIBAS
The private credit market raises many questions, and its lack of transparency is not helping. Official public data remain scarce. The available
estimates come mainly from private sources, whose methodologies vary widely. The Financial Accounts of the Federal Reserve (Fed) now shed

new light on the matter. They reveal that private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt through households (31% of funds’ total financing),
insurers and pension funds (20%), and banks (19%).
Private credit can take several forms. The most common is loans granted by closed-end funds 1 to small and medium-sized, unlisted and highly
indebted companies. These loans are generally variable-rate, with flexible terms based on cash flow rather than a measure of profitability. While

this asset class has inherent vulnerabilities 2 (Bank of France, 2025), it is also particularly well-suited to financing innovative and long-term
projects (such as digital 3, infrastructure, renewable energy and healthcare), especially when combined with an equity stake in the company. Its
slower growth in Europe likely explains, in part, the region’s lag in innovation financing.
Private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt…
Private credit has grown rapidly in the United States over the past decade and has established itself as an alternative to bank loans for

a category of borrowers. It now accounts for 17.3% of the total outstanding loans to American non-financial corporations (NFCs) 4 and 7.1%
1 Strictly speaking, a closed-end fund has a fixed term during which investors cannot withdraw their invested capital. However, the sector has diversified since 2020. The Fed distinguishes
between 1) strictly closed-end private funds (private debt funds), 2) business development companies, which may have an indefinite lifespan and allow for occasional redemptions of

shares, and 3) interval and tender offer funds, which offer the possibility of scheduled redemptions.
2 Notably, high credit risk, high leverage, difficulty in valuing loans, opaque credit ratings, occasionally complex credit structures, high concentration within the sector and increasing
liquidity risk.
3 In 2025, nearly half of the direct loans made by private credit funds went to the tech sector (software, data centres and artificial intelligence) (Abbassi et al., 2026).

4 No comparable data exist for the Eurozone. According to France Invest (2026) data, private credit issued by French funds accounted for 4% of annual bank credit flows to NFCs in 2025.
Who finances US private credit funds?

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 28 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
of their debt. Approximately one-third (35%) is managed by strictly closed-end funds, with another third (36%) by semi-open-end funds
that allow occasional early redemptions. The remaining third is held on the balance sheets of non-resident private debt funds (25%) and
securitisation vehicles (4%).
… through banks, insurers and pension funds, and households

In yet another example of the lengthening of the credit intermediation chain in the United States (following that of the mortgage market),
banks are among the main creditors of private credit funds (65% of their debt and 19% of their total financing). The low risk weighting
of loans granted by banks to these vehicles, compared to that of direct bank loans to non-financial corporations, is undoubtedly a factor
(Chernenko et al., 2024). The holdings of insurers and pension funds – consisting mainly of shares in strictly closed-end funds – make

them among the leading investors in private debt funds (30% of issued shares and 20% of total financing). This asset class offers them the
opportunity to diversify their portfolios and attractive returns. The rise of semi-open-end funds has expanded the investor base to include
retail investors (31% of total financing), albeit at the cost of increased liquidity risk, as evidenced by the significant wave of redemptions in
late 2025 and early 2026 5. American households directly hold 70% of the equity issued by semi-open funds and 22% of the shares issued

by strictly closed funds 6.
Abbassi, P., Aldasoro, I., and Doerr, S. (2026), Financing the digital economy: the role of private credit, BIS Quarterly Review, September
2026
Banque de France (2025), The reshaping of the credit landscape in light of the development of private debt and Its implications for
financial stability, Financial Stability Report, December 2025
Chernenko, S., Ialenti, R. and Scharfstein, D. (2024), Bank capital and the growth of private credit, Working paper

France Invest (2026), Private debt fund activity in France, March 2026
celine.choulet@bnpparibas.com
5 Triggered by concerns regarding private credit funds’ high exposure to the software sector and the quality of the loans granted.
6 This exposure is likely overestimated because, in the Fed’s financial accounts, the household sector includes individuals and non-profit institutions, as well as resident private
equity funds, which form partnerships with private credit funds. In order to align more closely with the European definition, households here include sole proprietors.

為今年獲利低點，但明年多項利潤率改善可期，維
持兩位數獲利成長。

《供應鏈瓶頸、產能不足對工業獲利成長形成逆風》
根據機構統計，標普500的83家工業公司已有79家
公司公布3Q26財報，有86%財報優於市場預期。
展望2027年，建築機械與重型卡車、航空國防、以
及電力設備等子產業受惠於製造業投資、國防支出
增加及電力基礎建設升級，仍有雙位數成長。不過
機構預估工業獲利年增將從2026年17.2%減緩至
15.5%。
航空國防、電力設備與工程建設等領域仍面臨供應
鏈瓶頸、產能擴充速度有限及專業技術人力短缺等
挑戰，導致獲利預估下調。另外，物流運輸、工程

機械及傳統工業自動化等子產業需求雖較上半年改
善，但企業資本支出復甦速度持續放緩，加上製造
業擴產進度不均，導致未來營收與獲利展望仍趨於
保守。因此，機構將工業產業4Q26、1Q27獲利年
增率下調到3.1%、7.4%。

《AI資料中心對電力與電氣化訂單成長放緩》
根據統計，全球燃氣渦輪機3Q26訂單量達27.1GW
（年增18%，低於上季71%)，其中奇異能源獲得
18.3W、西門子能源獲得1.8GW、三菱重工獲得
4.3GW。前三大燃氣渦輪機製造商更佔2026年上半
年訂單量90%，其中奇異能源比重分別達67%，三
菱重工與西門子能源則各佔15.8%與6.5%。奇異能
源3Q26財報受到電力與電氣化業務帶動營收略高於
市場預期。公司3Q26積壓訂單增加200億美元到
1,967億美元。公司同步將2026年營收從460億美
元上調到470億美元，自由現金流從120億美元上調

到165億美元。
《引擎供應瓶頸仍在，空巴維持商用飛機交付目標》
歐洲商用飛機製造龍頭空中巴士於10/28發布2Q26
財報，第二季商用飛機交付量為184架飛機比去年
同期，較去年同期大幅成長8%。由於引擎供應與部
分關鍵零組件仍為主要瓶頸，所以公司維持2026年
交付870架商用飛機目標與上季相同。
總結，工業產業受到航空運輸獲利不如預期、航太
國防供應鏈瓶頸、產能不足、專業技術人力缺工等
因素，導致機構下調工業產業4Q26、1Q27獲利成
長，所以本行對工業產業展望維持中立評等。
資料來源：JP Morgan，2026/11/11，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/24

## Document 492: ブラジル､ルラ氏の逆転再選から１ヵ月も｢トリプル安｣が続く ～財政不安と高金利の悪循環が相場の重しに､ルラ政権は市場の信_827d2271

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジル､ルラ氏の逆転再選から１ヵ月も｢トリプル安｣が続く ～財政不安と高金利の悪循環が相場の重しに､ルラ政権は市場の信_827d2271.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026年11月 24日

1/6 
ブラジル､ルラ氏の逆転再選から１ヵ月も｢トリプル安｣が続く 
～財政不安と高金利の悪循環が相場の重しに､ルラ政権は市場の信認を取り戻せるか～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 10月 25日のブラジル大統領選挙の決選投票でルラ大統領が逆転再選を果たしたことを受け
て、金融市場では株式、通貨レアル、国債の「トリプル安」が続いている。背景には、政権
交代による財政規律の強化を期待した投資家の持ち高解消に加え、次期ルラ政権の財政運営
に対する警戒感が影響している。 
○ ブラジルでは、公的債務残高が高水準にあるなか、高金利に伴う利払い負担が財政運営の余
地を狭めている。財政不安がリスクプレミアムの上昇やレアル安を招き、インフレ圧力を高
めることで利下げを困難にする一方、高金利が財政状況を一段と悪化させる悪循環が懸念さ
れる。金融市場では2027年の高インフレ・高金利・低成長を警戒する動きが強まっているほ
か、米国の高金利政策の長期化観測や原油安も相場の回復を妨げる一因となっている。 
○ こうしたなか、当局は国債の買い戻しなどを通じて市場の安定化を図るとともに、税優遇措
置の縮小などを柱とする財政調整法案の成立を目指している。政府は投資適格級の信用格付
けの回復を目指す方針も示している。ただし、ルラ氏は社会政策の維持・拡充に意欲を示し
ており、義務的歳出の抑制を含む財政健全化策の実効性と持続性については、金融市場との
間で認識の隔たりが残っている。 
○ 豊富な外貨準備や底堅い対内直接投資は対外ショックに対する緩衝材となるものの、相場が
持続的な回復基調に転じるには財政運営に対する信認の回復が不可欠である。先行きは、財
政調整法案の行方や2027年予算の内容、次期経済チームの人選と政策遂行能力、インフレ期
待の安定と利下げ余地の拡大が注目される。ルラ政権にとっては、社会政策の持続性を確保
するうえでも、財政不安と高金利の悪循環を断ち切ることができるかが問われている。

### Page 2

2/6 
【ルラ氏の逆転再選から約１ヵ月、選挙前の水準を回復できず「トリプル安」が続いている】 
10月25日のブラジル大統領選挙の決選投票では、ルラ大統領が有効投票の 51.79％を獲得し、フ
ラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員を破って
通算４期目の当選を果たした（注１）。
この結果を受けて、直後の金融市場で
は、主要株価指数（ボベスパ指数）が大
幅に下落し、取引の一時停止措置が発
動された。通貨レアルや国債にも売り
が広がり、選挙直後から３市場が同時
に調整する「トリプル安」の展開となっ
た（注２）。その後も相場の回復は限定
的であり、いずれも選挙前の水準を大
きく下回るなか、依然として売り圧力
がくすぶる展開が続いている（図１）。 
こうした金融市場の急変の背景を探るうえでは、第１回投票後に相場が上昇したことを振り返る
必要がある。第１回投票でフラビオ氏が事前予想に反して首位に立つと（注３）、投資家の間では、
政権交代による財政規律の強化と、それを通じた将来の金利低下への期待が強まった（注４）。こう
した動きは、実際の政策変更に先行する形で、ブラジル資産に対する市場の評価が高まっていたこ
とを意味する。しかし、そうした期待を覆す選挙結果を受けて、投資家による持ち高の解消が進ん
だことが相場の急落に繋がったと考えられる。その後も株価とレアル相場が選挙直後の水準を下回
っている背景には、先行きの財政・経済政策を巡る不確実性が残っていることがある。当初の相場
下落を引き起こした選挙結果を巡る事前の期待の剥落に加え、2027年１月に始まる次期任期の財
政運営に対する評価の見直しが、相場の回復を妨げていると考えられる。

【財政不安と高金利が相互に作用する形で、金融市場の調整を長引かせている】 
財政運営に対する警戒感は、選挙前からくすぶっていた。ブラジル中銀によれば、９月末時点の
公的債務残高はＧＤＰ比 82.9％に達している（図２）。さらに、９月までの 12ヵ月間の公的部門の
名目利払いはＧＤＰ比8.99％に上るなど、債務残高の大きさに加え、高金利が財政運営の余地を狭

注１ 10月26日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666866.html） 
注２ 10月27日付レポート「ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666918.html） 
注３ 10月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html） 
注４ 10月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html）

### Page 3

3/6 
めている。そのうえ、ルラ政権は選挙期間中に、貧困層向け給付制度（ボルサ・ファミリア）の給
付額引き上げなどを打ち出した。こうし
た措置は家計所得を支えることが期待
される一方、持続的な財源の確保には疑
問が残る。2027年の予算案では、基礎的
歳出に占める裁量的支出の割合が約
８％にとどまるとされており、歳出削減
による財政調整の影響が公共投資や行
政サービスに集中することも懸念され
る。こうしたことから、金融市場は、ル
ラ政権が社会政策を維持しつつ、義務的
歳出の増加をどこまで抑制できるかを
注視していると考えられる。 
財政運営に対する信認が低下すれば、投資家は国債などレアル建て資産を保有するリスクに対し
て、一段と高いリスクプレミアムを求めるようになる。さらに、レアル安は輸入物価の上昇を通じ
てインフレ圧力を高め、利下げを困難にする。一方、金利の高止まりは、国債の借り換えなどを通
じて利払い負担を増大させるとともに、民間需要の抑制を通じて税収にも下押し圧力を掛ける。こ
うした悪循環が強まれば、財政再建そのもののハードルが一段と高まることになる。足元では、高
金利にもかかわらずレアル相場が力強さを欠く展開となっている。背景には、投資家が先行きのレ
アル安や政策の不確実性を警戒するなか、高い金利収入を勘案しても、投資に伴うリスクに見合っ
た収益を得られないとの見方があると考えられる。

【金融市場では2027年の高インフレ・高金利・低成長を警戒する動きが強まっている】 
ブラジル中銀は、11月３～４日に開催した金融政策委員会（COPOM）において、政策金利を 13.75％
に据え置いた（注５）。委員会では、景気減速の動きが明確になる一方、インフレ期待の悪化や供給
面でのショックが政策運営を難しくしているとの認識が示された。供給ショックによる物価への二
次的な波及を警戒し、必要な金融引き締めを維持する姿勢を示している。 
足元のインフレ率が加速していることを受けて（図３）、その後に中銀が公表した市場予測調査
では、2027年のインフレ率及び政策金利の見通しが上方修正される一方、経済成長率の見通しは下
方修正された。市場では、依然として小幅な利下げが見込まれているものの、その余地は縮小して
いる。インフレ率の上振れと景気の下振れが同時に見込まれていることは、企業収益や資金調達環
境の双方にとって逆風となる。株式市場にとっては、企業収益の拡大に対する期待が後退する一方、
将来の利益を現在価値に換算する際の割引率も高止まりするという厳しい環境が続くことを意味

注５ 11月５日付レポート「ブラジル中銀は６会合ぶりに金利据え置き､ルラ再選による財政不安が影響」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/672403.html） 
図 2 公的債務残高の推移

### Page 4

4/6 
する。 
政府も最新の経済見通しにおいて、
2027年の経済成長率を2.3％から1.8％
へ引き下げる一方、インフレ率を3.8％
から 4.5％へ引き上げている。政府は市
場予想に比べて底堅い経済成長が続く
と見込んでいるものの、経済環境が厳し
さを増しているとの認識については共
通している。財政収支の改善を歳入増に
大きく依存する場合、景気の下振れによ
る税収の伸び悩みが財政健全化の取り
組みを難しくする可能性が高まる。

【米国の金利に対する見方や資源価格の動向も、ブラジル資産の回復を妨げる一因に】 
国内要因に加え、海外の金融市場環境も相場の回復を妨げる一因となっている。米国では、根強
いインフレなどを背景に、ＦＲＢ（米連邦準備制度理事会）による高金利政策が長期化する可能性
が高まっている。米ドル建て資産で高い利回りが得られる環境下では、投資家が新興国の通貨・信
用リスクを取る誘因は弱まりやすい。とりわけ、国内の財政運営に対する懸念がくすぶるブラジル
については、その影響が増幅されやすいと考えられる。 
資源輸出国であるブラジルにとって、国際商品市況の変動による影響は一様ではない。原油高は
輸出企業の収益を支える一方、国内の燃料価格や輸送費の上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を高める可能
性がある。反対に、原油安はインフレ抑制に向けた好材料となるものの、石油関連株には下押し圧
力が掛かる。実際、このところの原油安の動きはボベスパ指数の重しとなっている。 
なお、選挙直後の株式市場では、金利動向の影響を受けやすい内需関連株に売り圧力が集中する
一方、パルプや航空機関連など輸出企業の一角には株価が上昇する動きもみられた。株式市場全体
の動向を捉えるうえでも、金利上昇とレアル安が各業種の企業収益に与える影響の違いに留意する
必要がある。

【政府は市場安定化と財政調整を進めるも、政策の実効性と持続性が課題となる展開は変わらず】 
当局は金融市場の安定に向けた対応を進めてきた。国庫局は 10月 26日に固定金利国債と物価連
動国債の買い戻しを実施するとともに、通常の国債入札を一部中止した。こうした措置は、国債市
場の正常な機能を確保するための対応であり、市場流動性の低下を和らげる役割がある。 
財政政策を巡っても、政府はモレッティ企画予算相が主導する財政調整法案の成立に向けた取り
組みを進めている。同法案には、企業向け税優遇措置の縮小、税額控除の利用制限、予算執行の抑
制などが盛り込まれており、すでに議会上院（元老院）で可決されている。その後、議会下院（代
議院）でも法案本文が可決されたものの、報道によれば、修正案の採決などの手続きが残されてい

### Page 5

5/6 
るとされる。このため、現時点で法案が成立したわけではないことに注意が必要である。 
ドゥリガン財務相は、2030 年までに基礎的財政黒字をＧＤＰ比 1.5～2.0％に拡大させることに
より、投資適格級の信用格付けの回復を
目指す方針を表明している（図４）。ただ
し、現時点では政策方針の表明にとどま
っており、法定の財政目標そのものが変
更されたわけではない。他方、ドゥリガ
ン氏は３％のインフレ目標を維持する立
場を示すとともに、資本規制の導入も否
定している。こうした発言からは、金融
市場の安定に向けて、政策運営の予見可
能性を高めようとする姿勢がうかがえ
る。 
ルラ氏も、財政調整法案を支持する考えを示す一方、貧困層向けの支出を削減しない姿勢を強調
している。2027 年１月に始まる次期任期中に年金の受給開始年齢を引き上げることを否定すると
ともに、最低賃金を物価上昇率以上に引き上げる政策を継続する方針も表明した。ルラ政権は、税
優遇措置の見直しなどを通じて社会政策の維持と財政調整の両立を目指しているものの、こうした
政策の下で義務的歳出の増加をどこまで抑制できるかについては、政府と金融市場との間で認識の
隔たりが残りやすい状況にある。 
次期財務相を含む経済チームの人選も、先行きの政策遂行能力を見極める材料となる。人事に対
する金融市場の評価は、指名される人物の経歴のみならず、大統領の支持を得て予算・歳出改革を
推進できるかにも左右されることになる。政府の取り組みに対する市場の評価は一様ではなく、今
後の政策運営を通じて、ブラジル資産に上乗せされているリスクプレミアムを低下させることがで
きるかが、相場の行方を左右すると考えられる。

【先行きの焦点は、財政措置の着実な実行とそれを通じたインフレ期待の安定に】 
なお、ブラジル経済の対外ショックに対する耐性についても確認しておく必要がある。選挙前の
９月末時点における外貨準備高は 3,628億ドルに上るとともに、９月までの 12ヵ月間の対内直接
投資の流入額も経常赤字額を上回る。こうした対外収支や対外資産の状況は、国際金融市場の変動
に対する緩衝材となることが期待される。ただし、これらの数字は選挙後の資金動向を直接示すも
のではなく、財政運営に対する信認回復の必要性を低下させるものでもない。 
これらの材料を踏まえると、当面の金融市場では、財政対策や人事を巡る報道に反応するなど、
政策の実行力を見極める展開が続くと考えられる。相場が持続的な回復基調に転じるかを判断する
うえでは、①財政調整法案の行方、②2027年予算と財政健全化方針との整合性、③経済チームに付
与される権限と大統領による支持、④インフレ期待の安定と利下げ余地の拡大、などが重要な材料
となる。財政運営に対する信認が回復すれば、為替相場や長期金利の安定を通じて金融政策の自由

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
6/6
度が高まるとともに、株価にも好影響が及ぶ可能性がある。一方で、財政調整策の縮小や追加歳出、
レアル安の再加速が重なれば、高インフレ・高金利・低成長が長期化するリスクがある。ルラ政権
にとっては、選挙公約に掲げた社会政策を持続的に実施するためにも、物価や資金調達コストの安
定につながる財政運営を示すことができるかが問われている。
以 上

## Document 517: 今月のグラフ（2026年11月）銅価格、高値圏で推移

Source PDF: all report pdf/今月のグラフ（2026年11月）銅価格、高値圏で推移.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 11 月 5 日

調査レポート

今月のグラフ（2026 年 11 月）

銅価格、高値圏で推移

調査部 主任研究員 芥田知至

世界景気に敏感とされる銅価格が高値圏で推移している。国際指標となるロンドン金属取引所（LME）の 3 カ
月先物をみると、2026 年 9 月 10 日に 1 トンあたり 14,875 ドルと史上最高値を記録した。 
電気伝導性に優れる銅は、そもそも電気自動車や再生可能エネルギー向けの需要拡大に加えて、人工知能
（AI）・半導体に関連した需要増加も見込まれて、需給引き締まり観測が強まりやすかった。 
また、第 2 次トランプ政権の誕生後、米当局が銅製品などとともに銅地金の輸入にも、追加関税を賦課すると
の観測が浮上した。米国内で取引される銅の価格は関税分だけ高くなるとの思惑が働き、COMEX（ニューヨー
ク商品取引所）の銅価格を含めて、米国内で取引される銅価格は、国際的な価格よりも高くなった。このため、各
地から銅地金が米国に輸入されて、米国外の銅需給を引き締め、LME 銅相場にも押し上げ要因になった。 
最高値をつけた9 月10 日に、銅地金への追加関税について、米政権がまだ検討中と報じられると、銅のLME
相場は一時下落したが、その後も関税賦課観測は根強く 14,000 ドル台を中心に推移し、高止まりしている。 
銅価格と米国の主要半導体関連企業で構成されるフィラデルフィア半導体株指数（SOX）は、ともにエレクトロ
ニクス関連需要動向に敏感とみられ、連動性が強かったが、足元では SOX が下落気味なのに対して、銅価格は
高止まりしている。AI に対する期待の変動や長期金利高止まりのマイナス影響が SOX でより強く表れ、銅価格
は米関税賦課観測で支えられているためと思われる。

図表 銅価格（LME）とフィラデルフィア半導体株指数（SOX）の推移

（出所） Bloomberg

## Document 546: 凱基投顧_2610 華航_賴偉中_20261118

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_2610 華航_賴偉中_20261118.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 18, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 運輸 ‧ 台灣

華航 
(2610.TW/2610 TT)

2027年潛在獲利上行空間巨大 
重要訊息 
華航於 11/18 召開線上法人說明會。 
評論及分析 
用油成本下降，利差放大使 3Q26 獲利年增 69.6%。華航 3Q26 營收 651.1
億元、QoQ+2%，營業利益 66.7 億元、QoQ+150.4%。EPS 為 0.72 元、
QoQ+212.9%，EPS 為 0.72 元，低於預期 9.5%。3Q26 營收僅季增 2%，營
業利益卻大幅季增 150.4%，主要來自美伊衝突於 6 月趨緩後油價回落使用油
成本下降，同時先前 2Q26 調漲之燃油附加費於 3Q26 陸續實現，利差放大。 
4Q26 旺季營收將創歷史新高，不過獲利恐較去年衰退。4Q 為航空業全年
營運高峰，尤其貨運受惠於年底購物旺季需求挹注，AI 高科技半導體出口亦
保持強勁，預估華航 4Q26 營收 661.9 億元、YoY+22.2%，將創歷史新
高。不過，9 月美伊衝突再起導致油價上漲，預估平均用油成本將提升至
173 元/桶、QoQ+17.4%，抵銷營收成長，並使 EPS 年減 41.4%至 0.36
元。2026 全年 EPS 估為 2.19 元、YoY-10.4%。 
美國選民反戰態度確立，2027 年美伊戰爭淡化可能性高，華航獲利潛在上
行空間巨大。11 月美國期中選舉，選民已用選票清楚傳達反戰反通膨立
場，我們認為 2027 年美伊戰爭有望逐漸和緩化，油價具下行空間。目前我
們對 2027 年航空燃油成本假設為 150 元/桶，預估華航 EPS 為 2.17 元、
YoY-1.1%。根據凱基計算華航獲利對油價敏感度分析，如 2027 年航空燃
油成本降至 140 元/桶，華航 EPS 將可達 2.5 元以上。一旦美伊戰爭結束，
燃油成本恢復戰前水準(每桶 100 元以下)，獲利潛在上行空間將十分巨大。 
投資建議 
雖然 4Q26 獲利受油價影響將衰退，不過著眼於 2027 年美伊戰爭和緩可能使
油價下跌，將為華航創造巨大的潛在獲利成長空間，我們對華航保持正向看
法。凱基維持華航「增加持股」投資評等及目標價 26 元，計算基於 2027 年
底每股淨值之 1.5 倍，位於過去四年本淨比評價區間中值。 
投資風險 
中東衝突、航空燃油價格、飛安意外。

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

## Document 562: 凱基投顧_HPE Hewlett Packard Enterprise_張燾_20261124

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_HPE Hewlett Packard Enterprise_張燾_20261124.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 24, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 硬體‧美國

Hewlett Packard Enterprise 
(HPE.N/HPE US)

併購 Juniper效益顯現，FY2027 AI營收可望加速成長 
重要訊息 
HPE (HPE US)  FY4Q26 財報、FY1Q27 與 FY2027 財測皆優於市場共識，但
FY2027 AI 與雲端業務營利率預期較 FY4Q26 衰退，盤後股價下跌 7%。 
評論及分析 
FY4Q26 伺服器漲價與企業 AI 需求升溫，EPS 季增 29%至 1.43 美元。HPE 
FY4Q26 (2026/08-2026/10)營收年增 50.3%、季增 19.1%至 145.5 億美
元，主要動能來自伺服器漲價以及企業 AI 需求升溫，整體伺服器營收年增
94.7%、季增 29.9%至 87.9 億美元。FY4Q26 毛利率年增 1.8ppts、季減
2.2ppts 至 38.2%，季減主因為 AI 營收比重提升、稀釋毛利率；FY4Q26 
EPS 年增 129.5%、季增 28.4%至 1.43 美元，優於市場共識 11%。 
HPE 拓展資料中心互聯布局，預估 FY2027 AI 營收年增率提升至 59%。
HPE 在併購 Juniper 後擴大在 Scale-up 與 Scale-across 領域的佈局，並透過
Greenlake 平台提升軟硬體整合服務能力，提供客戶較完整的解決方案，使
公司在 AI 市場的接單狀況轉佳。隨 Vultr、X 與 TELUS 等 Neo-clouds 與企業
客戶拉貨量逐步升溫，凱基預估 HPE FY2027 AI 伺服器營收年增 59%至 94
億美元，年增率可望較 FY2026 之 31%提升。 
預估 FY2027 EPS 年增 41%至 5.61 美元。公司展望 FY2027 AI 與雲端產品
營利率為 14%，較 FY4Q26 之 18.4%下滑、低於市場預期，主因記憶體低
價庫存貢獻削減以及 AI 業務稀釋營利率；然而，隨 AMD 與 Oracle 對交換
器與網通產品拉貨可望於 FY2H27 升溫，仍有助 HPE FY2027 營利率維持年
增。此次凱基 EPS 預估大致不變，FY2027 EPS 預估年增 41%至 5.61 美
元，公司當前本益比回落至歷史區間中下緣之 8 倍，預期隨 AI 營收加速成
長與交換器產品放量，有助於公司評價回升至接近同業水準之 14 倍位階。 
投資建議 
凱基維持「增加持股」評等，目標價為 78 美元 (14x FY2027F EPS)。 
投資風險 
企業縮減資本支出影響 AI 伺服器訂單量；Juniper 併購綜效不如預期。

### Page 2

美國 Hewlett Packard Enterprise

十一月 24, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

HPE營收主要來自伺服器，AI營收占比約 17%。公司主要產品可拆分為: (1)
雲端與 AI 伺服器 (FY4Q26 營收占比約 77%) : 硬體方面提供 ProLiant 品牌
機架式/塔式伺服器，同時也包含 Apollo、Cray XD 等 HPC&AI 應用伺服器，
軟體方面則提供 HPE GreenLake Cloud 雲端介面管理工具與資安服務，以及
提供客戶彈性支付方案，包含借貸、融資等服務，其中通用型伺服器營收占
比為 43%、AI 伺服器營收占比約 17%，其餘為混合雲與金融服務；(2) 網
通 (Networking，佔營收比重為 21%) : 硬體端提供 Aruba 品牌之 WAP、
Switch、Routers 等網通產品，應用場域涵蓋校園、企業與網路分支環境；
(3) 企業投資與其他 (Corporate Investment and Others，佔營收比重為 2%) : 
包含 HP Labs 與業務孵化專案等。 
HPE 拓展網通布局與軟硬體整合能力，1.6T 交換器預計於 FY2H27 顯著貢
獻營收。HPE 相較 Dell (美)與 Super Micro ( 美)等同業的 AI 伺服器接單規模
較小，公司將重心放在獲利性較佳的主權 AI 與企業 AI，並透過併購 Juniper
以強化在交換器與路由器等產品的供應能力，從而提升在 AI 資料中心領域
的競爭力。另外，公司也推出 100%無風扇直接液冷系統架構的伺服器，可
使伺服器功耗降低 37% 並讓占地面積減少一半，並和 Nvidia 針對
GreenLake 平台共同開發軟體，透過結合軟硬體的方式以提升 AI 客戶忠誠
度。GreenLake 以雲端形式將整體基礎架構整合，並能將 AI 問題導向最合
適的模型，以減少 Token 消耗量，目前 ARR 達到 35 億美元，後續會再透過
導入更多模型以提升服務能力。 
HPE 推出業界首款採用 AMD Helios AI 平台的解決方案，HPE 將提供 AMD
搭載博通 Tomahawk 6 晶片的 HPE Juniper Networking  1.6T 交換器，而
AMD Herder 超級電腦也採用 HPE Cray GX5000 機櫃級系統，可因應參數量
達 1 兆以上的 AI 模型訓練需求，此產品預期在 FY2H27 開始顯著貢獻營
收。在 AI 帶動資料傳輸需求增加，以及交換器規格逐步升級至 800G/1.6T
帶動下，公司展望 FY2027 網通產品營收年增 18-22%，營利率則可望自
FY2026 約 22-23%提升至 25-28%。 
圖 1：HPE 推出搭載 Tomahawk 6晶片的 1.6T交換器

資料來源：公司資料；凱基

### Page 3

美國 Hewlett Packard Enterprise

十一月 24, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

凱基預估 HPE FY2027 AI伺服器營收年增 59%至 94億美元。HPE FY4Q26 
AI 新增訂單季增 84%至 57 億美元，AI 積壓訂單季增 38%至 105 億美元，
其中網通相關的金額達 34 億美元，公司以 FY2027 年底成長至 75-80 億美
元為目標，主要動能預期來自 AMD Helios 所採用的 1.6T 交換器與 Oracle
資料中心建置所需的網通產品。 
HPE AI 訂單約 70%來自 Blackwell，客戶則超過 60%屬於主權 AI 與企業，
隨 Vultr、X 與 TELUS 等 Neo-clouds 與企業客戶拉貨量逐步升溫，可望使
HPE AI 伺服器出貨維持年增趨勢，凱基預估 HPE FY2027 GB/VR 系列出貨櫃
數將自 FY2026 之 900 櫃提升至 1,800 櫃，可望推升整體 AI 伺服器營收年
增 59%至 94 億美元。獲利性的部分，GB300 過渡至 VR 伺服器雖預期使毛
利率稀釋 1-2%，但隨獲利性較佳的主權 AI 訂單貢獻逐步提升，預期 HPE 
AI 伺服器營利率將維持在 5%上下的水準。通用型伺服器的部分，受惠於 AI
推理需求提升，以及公司反應記憶體漲價成本而調整報價，預估
F2026/FY2027 營收達 193/283 億美元、年增 47%，營利率則預期隨低價記
憶體庫存貢獻逐步削減，自 FY4Q26 之 18%逐步回落至 10%-15%上下的水
準。 
HPE 評等維持「增加持股」，目標價 78 美元。HPE 在 AI 伺服器的競爭力在
於開發 Greenlake 平台以提升軟硬體整合能力與客戶忠誠度，並透過併購
Juniper 以提升在交換器與網通設備之布局完整度，有助於公司 AI 伺服器營
收之成長性逐步轉強並帶動獲利升溫；隨 AI 積壓訂單金額增加並預期將逐
步貢獻營收，凱基預估 HPE FY2027 AI 伺服器營收年增 59%至 94 億美元，
年增率可望較 FY2026 之 31%提升。HPE 在此次會議基於伺服器訂單狀況優
於預期而將 FY2027 營收年增率自約 15%上調至約 31%、優於凱基原預
估，故上調 HPE FY2027 營收預估約 8%至 637 億美元、年增 36%；但公司
預期 AI 與雲端產品線營利率將自 FY4Q26 之 18.4%回落至 14.0%，反應 AI
業務稀釋營利率與記憶體低價庫存貢獻逐步削減，低於凱基原預期，故下調
HPE 整體營利率預估約 0.6ppt 至 15.2%、年增 1.2ppts。在上調營收並下調
營利率預估下，凱基將 FY2027 EPS 自原預估之 5.39 美元上修至 5.61 美
元、年增 41%。相較市場共識預估 FY2027 EPS 為 4.73 美元，目前股價交
易於約 8/10 倍之 FY2027 凱基與市場共識 EPS 預估。 
HPE 過往五年本益比多落於 5-14 倍之間，考量 (1) HPE 拓展資料中心交換器
與路由器等網通產品布局，使公司能提供相對完整的解決方案，並有助於 AI
成長性逐步轉強；(2) HPE AI 產品之客戶有較高的比重屬於主權 AI 與企業用
戶，產品報價與營利率優於資料中心客戶約 1%-2%，AI 營收成長對公司獲
利貢獻度較高；(3) 隨 1.6T 交換器產品預期在 FY12027 開始銷售，以及合
併 Juniper 之效益逐步顯現，有助於整體獲利能力提升。綜合上述原因，預
期 HPE 本益比將回升至區間上緣之 14 倍上下浮動；基於當前評價偏低，故
維持「增加持股」之評等，目標價為 78 美元 (14x FY2027F EPS)。

### Page 7

美國 Hewlett Packard Enterprise

十一月 24, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  7

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

2
◆ドル円相場回顧
９月のドル円相場は、上旬に一時152円台後半
へドル安・円高が進行。日銀高官によるタカ派的な
発言をきっかけに、日銀による利上げペース加速への
思惑が強まったほか、米政府高官による円売りけん
制発言などが円を押し上げ。この間、投機筋による
円売りポジションが急速に巻き戻されたことも円高に
寄与。
下旬にかけて、159円台前半へドル高・円安が進
行。市場予想を上回る米経済指標を受け、ＦＲＢ
による早期利上げ観測が強まったことが背景。
10月入り後は、158円台前半を中心に横ばい圏
で推移。日銀が公表した「金融政策決定会合にお
ける主な意見」を受けて、日銀の早期利上げ観測が
後退した一方、米雇用統計が市場予想を下回り、

ＦＲＢによる早期利上げ観測も後退し、方向感が
定まらない展開に。
◆今後の見通し
当面のドル円相場は、横ばい圏で推移する見通し。
資源高によるインフレ懸念の高まりからＦＲＢ・日銀
ともに利上げを継続し、米日金利差が大きく変わら
ないとみられることが背景。
来春以降は、緩やかにドル安・円高が進行する見
通し。ＦＲＢは利上げによる景気や物価への影響
を見極めるため、政策金利を据え置く一方、日銀は
来年半ばにかけて四半期に一度のペースで利上げ
を継続し、米日金利差が縮小することが背景。
ドル円相場見通し：来春以降、緩やかな円高へ

為替
(株)日本総合研究所 為替相場展望 2026年10月
◆ユーロ相場回顧
９月のユーロドル相場は、上旬に1.16ドル台前半
を中心に横ばい圏で推移。ユーロ圏のインフレ率が
前月から上昇した一方、米雇用統計が市場予想を
上回り、ＥＣＢ・ＦＲＢともに早期利上げ観測が
強まったことが背景。その後、10月上旬にかけて、一
時1.11ドル台半ばへユーロ安・ドル高が進行。堅調
な米経済指標がドル高圧力となったほか、フランスの
財政不安がユーロ安圧力に。
対円では、上旬に日銀高官によるタカ派的な発言
を受けて、177円台後半へ円高が進行。中旬には、
日銀の早期利上げ観測が後退したことから、一時
181円台半ばへ円安が進行。その後、10月上旬に
かけてフランスの予算編成を巡る財政悪化懸念から、
一時176円台前半へユーロが下落。
◆今後の見通し
先行きユーロドル相場は、緩やかなユーロ安基調
で推移する見通し。当面はフランスの財政不安が重
石となるほか、来春にかけてＥＣＢは政策金利を据
え置く一方、ＦＲＢは追加利上げを実施するとみら

れることが背景。もっとも、来年半ば以降は利上げが
打ち止めとなり、横ばい圏で推移する見込み。
対円では、緩やかなユーロ安基調で推移する見通
し。当面はフランスの財政悪化懸念がユーロの重石
となるほか、来年半ばにかけては、日銀の段階的な
追加利上げに伴う日欧金利差の縮小が円高圧力
に。

（年/月）
日本総研予測
◆過去の選挙後、ドル円の方向はまちまち
11月に実施される米中間選挙を控え、下院は民
主党が優勢との予測が大勢。民主党が過半数を奪
還した場合、政権と議会多数派が異なる「分割政
府」となる見込み。過去10回の中間選挙でも、分
割政府は９回と大半。
もっとも、過去の選挙後のドル円相場はドル高・ド
ル安の双方へと推移しており、一定の傾向はみられ
ず。選挙結果だけから、先行きのドル円相場の方向
性を見通すことは困難。
◆方向性を左右するのは日米金融政策
先行きのドル円相場を見通すうえでは、日米金融
政策が重要な要因のひとつ。中間選挙後に顕著に
円高が進んだ2022年も、米ＣＰＩの下振れを受

けたＦＲＢの利上げペース減速観測や、日銀の
イールドカーブ・コントロール政策の修正などにより、
米日金利差が縮小したことが背景。
当面のドル円相場は、足元の円安圏で推移する
見通し。来春にかけて、資源高によるインフレ懸念の
高まりからＦＲＢ・日銀ともに利上げを継続するとみ
られ、米日金利差が大きく変わらないことが背景。

米中対立の常態化や米国の自国第一主義の強まりを背景に、既存の国際経済秩序が揺らいでいます。こうしたなか、各国は存
在感を高めるグローバルサウス諸国との外交・経済関係の強化を急いでいます。日本も、OECD（経済協力開発機構）などと協調し
ながら、関係強化を目指していますが、現状はASEANやインドなどに偏重しており、アフリカや中南米との連携は限定的です。本シン
ポジウムでは、はじめに世界経済の構造変化や日本のグローバルサウス諸国との連携策の現状・課題を多角的に検証します。その
上で、日本がグローバルサウス諸国との関係を深化させるための具体的な戦略を議論します。

MSCI 亚太指数下跌 2% ，抹去周⼆创下 7 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇涨幅的全部涨势。⽇股半导体封装
基板⻰头揖斐电电⼦单⽇暴跌 17% ，存储芯⽚巨头铠侠重挫 15% 。⽇本第⼀⼤半导体设备
巨头东京电⼦⼤跌  13.09% ，硅晶圆⼤⼚胜⾼亦下挫 12.23% 。 WTI 原油跌超 1.4% ，报 80.8
美元。
摘要：
亚太股市周三⼤幅下跌，此前美国科技股遭遇重创，市场对⼈⼯智能投资能否带来⾜够回报的
疑虑再度升温。与此同时，投资者还需消化潜在贸易消息影响。
周三，韩国综合股价指数⼤跌 5.4% ，领跌亚太市场。⽇本⽇经 225 指数跌逾 3% 。 MSCI 亚太指数
下 跌 2% ， 抹 去 周 ⼆ 创 下 7 ⽉ 以 来 最 ⼤ 单 ⽇ 涨 幅 的 全 部 涨 势 。 中 国 台 湾 地 区 股 指 下 跌 8% ⾄
36,892.58 点。
此轮抛售延续了华尔街的跌势，隔夜纳斯达克综合指数下滑 1.5% ，英伟达单⽇跌幅近 6% ，费城
半导体指数重挫 7.9% ，⾃ 6 ⽉⾼点以来累计跌幅已达约 40% 。

市场⽬光现已转向周三美国将公布的个⼈消费⽀出（ PCE ）价格指数，以及潜在的关税措施，
两者均有可能影响美联储的政策路径。
科技股抛售重挫亚太股市
隔夜美股英伟达及费城半导体指数掉头向下，打断了亚太科技股的反弹节奏，资⾦从前⼀⽇⼤
幅拉升的半导体设备、存储芯⽚及数据中⼼光通信赛道快速撤离。
周三韩国综合股价指数领跌亚太市场，跌幅扩⼤⾄ 5.4% ，此前韩国交易所启动了 SIDECAR 机
制，暂时暂停程序化卖盘。

### Page 3

此轮亚洲科技股的抛售直接追踪美国隔夜⾛势。英伟达周⼆再度承压，芯⽚设计公司 Ambarella
在美股盘后交易中跌幅更⾼达 37% ，原因是其第四季度营收指引仅为 7000 万⾄ 7500 万美元，远
低于分析师预期的约 1.12 亿美元。
EP Wealth Advisors 投资组合策略董事总经理 Adam Phillips 表⽰：
他表⽰⽬前尚未逢低买⼊。
市场质疑 AI 资本⽀出可持续性
此轮波动的核⼼，是市场对 AI 投资回报前景的⽇益审视。过去三个交易⽇，股市剧烈震荡：上周
五 AI 交易全⾯崩跌，周⼀因英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋复职消息提振⽽强劲反弹，周⼆涨势随即再度回
吐。
嘉盛理财⾸席投资策略师 Liz Ann Sonders 在接受 CNBC 采访时指出， AI ⽣态系统的抛售早于有
关⻩仁勋及英伟达的消息出现。 Liz Ann Sonders 表⽰：
Bank J. Safra Sarasin 亚洲⾸席投资官 Dong Chen 在接受彭博电视采访时表⽰：
避险情绪推升美债，⻩⾦⼩幅反弹

股票市场的动荡促使部分资⾦流⼊避险资产。美国 10 年期国债收益率横盘于 4.90% ，延续纽约
交易时段⾛势。
（市场）每天都令⼈眼花缭乱。从周⼀到现在，基本⾯并没有发⽣根本性变化，但我们还未⾛出
困境。围绕 AI 交易的恐慌与不确定性仍需消化。
资本⽀出的担忧、数据中⼼的建设、资本成本与投资回报率之间的背离，以及市场内部盈利与亏
损板块之间的分化 —— 这才是更宏观、更核⼼的故事。
市场告诉我们的是，投资者认为，此前所指引的投资规模可能难以按原计划推进，⾄少⽆法以可
持续的⽅式维系。

续升值趋势的可能性不⼤ " ，预计美元兑⽇元将在 155 ⾄ 165 区间内运⾏。

1 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
景気は底堅い
（出所）米商務省 （出所）米供給管理協会
米国経済は堅調に推移している。7-9月期の実質GDP成長率は前期比年率3.4%に加速した。個人消費が2期連続で同
3.8%と好調に推移したほか、設備投資は同12.7%増と、コロナ禍の極端な落ち込みからの反動増がみられた2020年7-9
月期以来6年ぶりの高い伸びを記録した。企業の景況感を示すISM景況指数は、製造業、非製造業いずれも判断の目安
となる50の水準を上回った。先行き、堅調な個人消費や旺盛なAI需要などから景気は底堅く推移する見込みである。

（注） 2025年10月の失業率は欠損値

生産、雇用は増加
（出所）米FRB
 9月の鉱工業生産は前月比0.7%増加した。自動車・部品が同2.8%減少したが、電力などの公益が同3.5%増加した。ま
た、設備投資動向を示す9月のコア資本財出荷は同1.2%増と8カ月連続で増加した。
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差2.9万人増加した。業種別では、教育・医療（同2.0万人増）、建設（同1.1万人増）の
増加幅が比較的大きかったが、政府（同1.7万人減）、情報（同1.1万人減）などが減少し、雇用全体の伸びを抑制した。
一方、失業率は4.2%と、およそ1年ぶりの水準に低下した前月から小幅悪化した。

消費は横ばい、物価上昇率はガソリン高が懸念
（出所）米商務省
 9月の実質個人消費は前月比横ばいであった。内訳をみると、自動車などの耐久財消費が同0.8%減少、ガソリンなどの
非耐久財消費も横ばいであったが、外食、宿泊などのサービス消費が同0.2%増加し全体を下支えした。
 消費者物価（エネルギーと食料品を除くコアベース）上昇率は、9月は前年比2.8%と前月と同じ上昇率であった。一方、
FRBが重視する個人消費支出物価指数（同）上昇率は、9月は同3.1%に加速した。先行き、サービスは鈍化傾向にある
が、財は関税に加え、ガソリン価格上昇の影響もあり、物価上昇圧力が高まる懸念がある。

住宅：着工は減少、価格は上昇
（出所）米商務省 （出所）S&P Global

5 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
AI関連の投資リスク
 IMFは10月に公表した国際金融安定性報告書で、金融市場におけるAI関連投資において、投資レバレッジ拡大や投資集
中により、価格調整が起きた場合に影響が増幅されるリスクや、AI関連企業の将来の債務返済リスクを指摘した。
 報告書によると、レバレッジ型ETFが行う投資が、少数のAI関連銘柄に集中度を高めているほか、ヘッジファンドがAI関連
株式への投資配分を高める動きもみられる。いずれも、株安局面で株価下落を加速させる要因になる。また、米プラット
フォーム企業など「ハイパースケーラー」と呼ばれるデータセンター運営企業が債券発行を増加させるなど、AI関連企業が
債務を拡大させており、収益が想定を下回る場合の債務返済リスクを高めている。
 報告書はまた、こうしたリスク増大にもかかわらず、日本を含む世界が米株投資を拡大させていることに懸念を示した。

## Document 755: 統一-Nvidia(NVDA.US)-20261113

Source PDF: all report pdf/統一-Nvidia(NVDA.US)-20261113.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1
